```text
1	\section{Discrimination Collapse Details}
2	\label{app:discrimination-collapse-details}
3	
4	This cross-model consistency (Table~\ref{tab:discrimination-metrics-full}) establishes discrimination collapse as a robust consequence of compound sparsity. When importance scores lose discriminative power, thresholding the bottom-$s$ fraction becomes noise-sensitive: small perturbations change which positions cross the eviction boundary. In the limit of perfectly uniform scores, importance-based eviction degenerates into an arbitrary selection functionally equivalent to biased random eviction. The same pattern holds under random eviction (DeepSeek: $-0.031$ to $-0.073$; Qwen: $-0.019$ to $-0.045$), confirming that discrimination collapse is a consequence of head pruning \emph{per se}, not of the eviction strategy. % exp_id: c4_entropy_deepseek, c4_entropy_qwen
5	
6	
7	\section{Extended Discussion}
8	\label{app:extended-discussion}
9	
10	\paragraph{On the predictability of phase-modulated severity.}
11	The compound interaction is not uniformly fragile---it is selectively fragile in a phase-dependent manner ($\widetilde{\mathrm{II}}_{\text{high}} < 0.06$ at $s \leq 0.30$). % exp_id: bf16_importance_s015..s030; high-phase medians: 0.006, 0.015, 0.032, 0.053
12	
13	\paragraph{Random masking as falsification control.}
14	Random eviction fails 7/8 paired permutation tests across both models; the single exception (Qwen $s{=}0.30$, $p{=}0.012$) exhibits negligible effect size ($\widetilde{\Delta} = \placeholder{}$). The absence of a phase gradient under random masking isolates the amplification as strategy-specific. % exp_id: bf16_ds_rand_s015..s030, bf16_qw_rand_s015..s030
15	
16	\paragraph{Entropy mechanism boundary conditions.}
17	Directly measuring per-head entropy shifts across architecturally distinct models---and identifying what training-time properties determine whether entropy concentrates or disperses under head pruning---would clarify the boundary conditions of the full mechanism.
18	
19	
20	\section{Extended Limitations}
21	\label{app:limitations}
22	
23	\paragraph{Model scale.}
24	All experiments are conducted at the 7B parameter scale.
25	Whether the phase-modulated severity patterns persist, amplify, or attenuate at larger scales (e.g., 70B) remains untested.
26	Larger models may exhibit different attention redundancy structures that alter the discrimination collapse dynamics (and the model-dependent entropy component may behave differently at scale).
27	
28	
29	\paragraph{Architectural scope.}
30	Our two models---DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-7B and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct---share the Qwen2ForCausalLM architecture.
31	The cross-model replication (\S\ref{sec:cross-model}) establishes that the findings are not artifacts of a single model's weights, though both models share the same Qwen2ForCausalLM architecture.
32	Preliminary cross-architecture validation on DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Llama-8B (LlamaForCausalLM, \S\ref{sec:cross-arch}) shows qualitatively consistent within-importance phase gradients ($d_z \sim 1.0$) but a substantially weaker strategy $\times$ phase interaction than in Qwen2-based architectures; random pruning also exhibits a non-trivial phase gradient on LLaMA, reducing the strategy contrast. This partial confirmation ($s{=}0.30$ Wilcoxon $p_{\text{Bonf}}{=}0.029$; $s \leq 0.25$ non-significant) suggests the qualitative pattern may generalize but quantitative impact is architecture-dependent. Comprehensive cross-architecture validation across more diverse designs (e.g., Mistral, multi-latent attention architectures) and scales remains an important next step.
33	
34	
35	\paragraph{Task coverage.}
36	We evaluate exclusively on MATH-500, a mathematical reasoning benchmark.
37	Different reasoning tasks---code generation, commonsense reasoning, multi-hop question answering---may induce different phase structures with different sparsity distributions, potentially altering the severity gradient profile.
38	Generalizing the phase-strategy interaction to broader reasoning domains is an important next step.
39	
40	
41	\paragraph{Statistical scope.}
42	Bootstrap confidence intervals use $B{=}10{,}000$ resamples (recomputed from initial $B{=}1{,}000$ profiling runs). The paired permutation test yields $n{=}49$ paired problems for DeepSeek (1 excluded) and $n{=}46$ for Qwen (4 excluded). % exp_id: cohens_d_bf16.json, cohens_d_bf16_qwen.json
43	The severity difference distribution is right-skewed (mean/median ratio ${\sim}1.6{:}1$ at $s{=}0.30$), motivating our use of medians and nonparametric tests rather than parametric alternatives. % exp_id: bf16_importance_s030; mean_diff=22.94, median_diff=14.59 (segment-level). Per-problem paired: median_diff=9.9436 at s030
44	All four permutation tests reject $H_0$ after Bonferroni correction ($p_{\text{Bonf}} < 0.005$; Table~\ref{tab:permutation}), with CI lower bounds increasing from $0.19$ to $6.70$---confirming the effect direction at every sparsity level. The permutation CIs at higher sparsity are wider (e.g., $[6.70,\; 16.29]$ at $s{=}0.30$), reflecting genuine variance in per-problem severity amplification. % exp_id: permutation_test_results.json; permutation CIs: s015=[0.19,0.62], s020=[0.69,1.49], s025=[1.66,6.64], s030=[6.70,16.29]
45	
46	A notable exception among the random baselines is the Qwen random condition at $s{=}0.30$, which yields a statistically significant result ($p{=}0.012$) despite negligible effect size ($\widetilde{\Delta} = \placeholder{}$, bootstrap CI spanning zero). % exp_id: qw_rand_s030; B=10000, p=0.012, d=-0.035, negligible
47	This case illustrates that dichotomous significance testing can flag effects of no practical magnitude---reinforcing the importance of reporting effect sizes and confidence intervals alongside $p$-values.
48	
49	\paragraph{Sparsity range.}
50	Our experiments span $s \in \{0.15, 0.20, 0.25, 0.30\}$, a moderate range representative of practical deployment targets.
51	More extreme sparsity ($s > 0.50$) may exhibit qualitatively different interaction dynamics---the sub-exponential growth pattern suggests the severity gradient may plateau, but this remains unverified.
52	Conversely, the lower boundary at which phase-modulated amplification first emerges is not yet characterized.
53	
54	
55	\paragraph{No throughput evaluation.}
56	We do not report inference throughput or latency, which are necessary for deployment analysis but orthogonal to our severity characterization.
57	
58	% Entropy redistribution analysis moved to main text \S\ref{sec:entropy-redistribution}
59	
```

Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. **背景**：Phase 1 framing/写作修复已完成。本指令填充所有 data placeholders（n=500）并写入补充分析结果。所有数据文件在本地 `artifacts/n500_analysis/` 下。

---

## 数据源

Paper Agent 请从以下文件读取数据（**统一数据源，不要从 registry 读 Qwen 数据——registry Qwen 是 n=274 旧值**）：

1. **`artifacts/n500_analysis/n500_unified_final.csv`** — 16 conditions 完整数据（DS n=496, QW n=475）
   - 含 `bonf_p_k4`（主报告用）、`fdr_p_bh`（robustness）、`bonf_p_k48`（旧值参考）
   - `significance` 列基于 `bonf_p_k4`

2. **`artifacts/n500_analysis/n500_full_statistics.md`** — 完整 16 条件统计摘要

3. **`artifacts/n500_analysis/supplementary_stats.json`** + **`supplementary_stats.md`** — 5 项补充分析：
   - CI width shrinkage（n=50→n=500）
   - BH-FDR 校正
   - Jonckheere-Terpstra test
   - G ratio bootstrap CI
   - Spearman 单尾/双尾确认

4. **`artifacts/n500_analysis/qwen_midphase_null_analysis.md`** — Qwen mid-phase 45.2% null 根因

5. **`artifacts/n500_analysis/missing_references.md`** — PM-KVQ/RLKV/LongFlow 引用评估

6. **`artifacts/n500_analysis/min_phase_length_sensitivity.csv`** — mpl=5/10/20 × 16 conditions robustness

---

## 需要填充/修改的内容

### A. 填充所有 \placeholder{} 和空白数据（MF1）

**Abstract**：
- 用 n=500 数据更新 headline numbers（DS median diff: 0.39→1.21→3.99→8.48；QW: 0.07→0.31→1.07→3.16）
- Random masking：补上 Qwen random s030 的 Δ̃ 值
- 所有 p-value 用 `bonf_p_k4`

**§5.4 Cross-model**：
- 填充 NULL_PCT、N_LLAMA placeholder
- n=500 updated numbers

**§5.5 LLaMA Table 4**：
- Phase 1 已将此表降级为 "exploratory"。如果表格结构仍在论文中，用已有 LLaMA n=50 数据填充（LLaMA 没有 n=500，只有 n=50）
- 如果 Phase 1 已删除 Table 4，确认不需要填充

**§7 Discussion — oracle pilot**：
- 填充 ORACLE_PCT、ORACLE_P、HEUR_PCT、HEUR_P（来自 c5b_pilot 实验数据：oracle recovery 18%, Wilcoxon p=0.002; heuristic 32%, p=0.024, 但 95% CI 均跨零）

**Appendix**：
- Qwen effective-n table 填充
- Per-problem concordance ρ/p 填充
- LLaMA random p-value 填充

### B. Random masking threshold story（REV-ADD-2）

§5 Results 中需要明确呈现 D063 threshold story：
- s015/s020: random NS（两模型一致）
- s025: DS bonf_p_k4=0.006, QW bonf_p_k4<1e-9 → 跨模型显著
- s030: DS bonf_p_k4=0.006, QW bonf_p_k4<1e-9
- Importance vs Random 效应量差异：DS 15-39×, QW 2.7-20×
- Framing：**"random masking develops a detectable but weak phase gradient above a sparsity threshold ~s020-s025; importance masking amplifies this effect by an order of magnitude"**
- **不要**写 "random = no phase gradient"（旧叙事，C019 已 deprecated）

### C. 统计方法论更新（MF4 + SF3 + SF6 + SF7）

**§4 Methodology**：
- **Bonferroni 说明**：family-wise k=4（每个 model×strategy 组合内 4 sparsity levels）。论证：这 4 个检验是同一 dose-response 假设的不同水平。BH-FDR 作为全局 robustness check 在 Appendix 报告
- **CI width shrinkage**：n=50 → n=500 CI 宽度缩窄 2.5-5.9×，作为 precision gain 证据（不用 "post-hoc power" 这个术语）
- **排除标准**：DS n_effective=496（4 problems null II），QW n_effective=475（25 problems null low/high phase）。Qwen mid-phase 45.2% null（226/500）源于短 reasoning trace 的 tercile 分割无法产生 mid segment，不影响 D = low−high 计算
- **Spearman 修正**：标注 "Spearman ρ=1.0, exact permutation p=0.042 (one-tailed, n=4 levels)"

**Dose-response monotonicity**：
- 补充 Jonckheere-Terpstra test 结果（从 supplementary_stats.json 读取）
- 明确区分 importance 和 random 的 monotonicity：both ρ=1.0 but effect sizes differ 3-39×
- Framing："Both strategies exhibit monotonic dose-response, but importance masking effect sizes are 3-39× larger"

### D. G ratio 处理（SF2）

- G 降级为 auxiliary metric（"amplification ratio"），Δ（paired median diff）为 primary
- 报告 G bootstrap CI（从 supplementary_stats.json）
- 特别注意：importance G CI 显著排除 1；random G 6/8 CI 排除 1 但 magnitude trivial (<1.4×) vs importance (22-148×)
- QW imp s020 G CI [0.47, 5.08] 含 1——标注为 "evidence weaker at this condition"

### E. BH-FDR Appendix（SF3）

- Appendix 新增一段或一个表：BH-FDR 校正 p-values 对比 Bonferroni k=4
- 结论预期：BH-FDR 结论与 Bonferroni k=4 一致（所有 Bonferroni 显著的在 FDR 下也显著）

### F. min_phase_length robustness（SF1 补充）

- Appendix 新增表格：mpl=5/10/20 × 16 conditions 的 median_diff + bonf_p
- 一句话结论："Core findings are robust to min_phase_length variation (5, 10, 20); importance phase gradients remain significant across all conditions. Random masking at s030 becomes borderline under mpl=20 due to sample loss but direction is preserved."

### G. 新引用（SF8）

从 `missing_references.md` 添加：
- **PM-KVQ** (ICLR 2026 Poster)：Related Work + Discussion。"PM-KVQ's progressive mixed-precision quantization along the temporal axis resonates with our finding that compound severity is phase-dependent"
- **RLKV** (arXiv 2510.08525)：Related Work。"RLKV applies reinforcement learning to optimize per-head KV cache retention"
- **LongFlow** (arXiv 2603.11504, withdrawn)：Related Work，标注 preprint。"LongFlow addresses KV cache compression for long-output reasoning models"
- Bib 条目：LongFlow 标注 `note = {arXiv preprint, withdrawn from ICLR 2026}`

### H. D064 d_z caveat 简化

n=500 下 D064 anomaly（DS s020→s025 d_z variance explosion）已消失。论文中如有 "d_z 降为辅助指标" 的强限定，可简化为 footnote："At n=50, d_z exhibited instability at high sparsity due to outlier-driven variance; this resolved at n=500."

1	# Supplementary Statistical Analyses (Reviewer Response)
2	
3	Date: 2026-05-15 | Data: n=500 (n_valid≈475-496) | Bootstrap B=10,000 | Seed=42
4	
5	---
6	
7	## 1. CI Width Shrinkage (n=50 → n=500)
8	
9	Confidence interval widths narrowed 2.5–5.9× across all 16 conditions, replacing post-hoc power analysis with direct precision evidence.
10

12	|-----------|----------------|-----------------|-----------|

14	| DS imp s020 | 0.6005 | 0.2302 | 2.6× |
15	| DS imp s025 | 2.4627 | 0.8211 | 3.0× |
16	| DS imp s030 | 8.2613 | 1.9306 | 4.3× |
17	| DS rand s015 | 0.2046 | 0.0544 | 3.8× |
18	| DS rand s020 | 0.3824 | 0.0925 | 4.1× |

24	| QW imp s030 | 1.9288 | 0.7748 | 2.5× |

26	| QW rand s020 | 0.3687 | 0.0829 | 4.4× |
27	| QW rand s025 | 1.6220 | 0.3257 | 5.0× |
28	| QW rand s030 | 3.0729 | 0.6361 | 4.8× |
29	
30	**Mean shrinkage: 3.7×. This demonstrates that n=500 provides substantially narrower CIs, directly addressing precision concerns without relying on post-hoc power analysis.**
31	
32	---
33	
34	## 2. Benjamini-Hochberg FDR Correction
35	
36	44 tests total across 4 families. BH-FDR recovers 5 additional significant results compared to Bonferroni (37 vs 32 significant).
37	
38	### Key result: DS random s025/s030 recover significance under BH-FDR
39	
40	| Test | Raw p | BH q | BH sig? | Bonferroni p | Bonf sig? |
41	|------|-------|------|---------|-------------|-----------|
42	| DS rand_s025 Wilcoxon | 1.46e-03 | 1.91e-03 | **Yes** | 6.42e-02 | No (borderline) |
43	| DS rand_s030 Wilcoxon | 1.48e-03 | 1.91e-03 | **Yes** | 6.49e-02 | No (borderline) |
44	| DS rand_s025→s030 adjacent | 1.78e-03 | 2.24e-03 | **Yes** | 7.84e-02 | No |
45	| cross_model rand_s025 | 1.51e-02 | 1.79e-02 | **Yes** | 6.63e-01 | No |
46	| cross_model rand_s030 | 3.94e-03 | 4.82e-03 | **Yes** | 1.73e-01 | No |
47	
48	### Summary by test family
49	
50	| Family | Total | BH sig | Bonf sig |
51	|--------|-------|--------|----------|
52	| Per-condition Wilcoxon | 16 | 12 | 10 |
53	| Cross-model Mann-Whitney | 8 | 6 | 4 |
54	| Importance vs Random | 8 | 8 | 8 |
55	| Adjacent sparsity | 12 | 11 | 10 |
56	
57	**The Bonferroni correction with n_tests=48 (original) was overly conservative. BH-FDR at α=0.05 controls the false discovery rate while recovering the borderline DS random s025/s030 results, which are now clearly significant (q < 0.002).**
58	
59	---
60	
61	## 3. Jonckheere-Terpstra Test (Dose-Response)
62	
63	Tests whether per-problem D = ii_low − ii_high increases monotonically across 4 sparsity levels. Uses raw per-problem values (~496 per group), far more powerful than 4-point Spearman.
64	
65	| Group | z | p | Medians (s015→s030) | Significant |
66	|-------|---|---|---------------------|-------------|
67	| DS importance | 33.73 | < 1e-200 | 0.39, 1.21, 3.99, 8.48 | Yes (***) |
68	| DS random | 6.22 | 2.50e-10 | 0.01, 0.03, 0.26, 0.51 | Yes (***) |
69	| QW importance | 30.10 | < 1e-200 | 0.07, 0.31, 1.07, 3.16 | Yes (***) |
70	| QW random | 10.92 | < 1e-200 | -0.01, 0.02, 0.39, 0.91 | Yes (***) |
71	
72	**All 4 groups show highly significant monotonic dose-response (all p < 1e-9). The JT test, using ~496 observations per sparsity level rather than 4 aggregated medians, provides overwhelming evidence of dose-response that the 4-point Spearman (p=0.042) could only suggest.**
73	
74	---
75	
76	## 4. G (Amplification Ratio) Bootstrap CI
77	
78	G = ii_low / ii_high per problem. G > 1 indicates low-sparsity phase experiences greater compound interaction than high-sparsity phase. Bootstrap B=10,000, 95% CI.
79	
80	### Importance masking (filtered: both ii_low > 0 and ii_high > 0)

83	|-----------|---------|----------|--------|-------------|
84	| DS imp s015 | 275 | 30.3 | [26.1, 37.3] | Yes |
85	| DS imp s020 | 287 | 61.7 | [50.1, 72.5] | Yes |
86	| DS imp s025 | 333 | 99.8 | [85.9, 120.4] | Yes |
87	| DS imp s030 | 363 | 147.8 | [119.1, 171.4] | Yes |
88	| QW imp s015 | 146 | 22.2 | [16.3, 34.2] | Yes |
89	| QW imp s020 | 204 | 45.7 | [34.0, 62.3] | Yes |
90	| QW imp s025 | 275 | 83.7 | [61.4, 95.8] | Yes |
91	| QW imp s030 | 309 | 110.5 | [81.0, 134.4] | Yes |
92	
93	### Random masking (filtered: |ii_high| > 0.001)
94	
95	| Condition | n_valid | Median G | 95% CI | Excludes 1? |
96	|-----------|---------|----------|--------|-------------|
97	| DS rand s015 | 493 | 0.52 | [0.40, 0.61] | Yes (<1) |
98	| DS rand s020 | 492 | 0.76 | [0.65, 0.89] | Yes (<1) |
99	| DS rand s025 | 496 | 1.09 | [0.96, 1.20] | No |
100	| DS rand s030 | 496 | 1.15 | [1.05, 1.28] | Yes (>1) |
101	| QW rand s015 | 469 | 0.67 | [0.44, 0.78] | Yes (<1) |
102	| QW rand s020 | 472 | 0.88 | [0.74, 1.02] | No |
103	| QW rand s025 | 471 | 1.26 | [1.17, 1.37] | Yes (>1) |
104	| QW rand s030 | 472 | 1.30 | [1.20, 1.39] | Yes (>1) |
105	
106	**Importance masking: G ranges from 22–148× (all CIs far above 1), confirming massive phase-dependent amplification. G increases with sparsity, showing the amplification effect intensifies at higher sparsity. Random masking G ranges 0.5–1.3; while statistically significant in 6/8 conditions, the magnitude is trivial (< 1.4×) compared to importance masking (22–148×), indicating that phase asymmetry under random masking is negligible in practical terms.**
107	
108	---
109	
110	## 5. Spearman ρ Single-Tail / Two-Tail Confirmation
111	
112	With n=4 ordered sparsity levels and ρ=1.0 for all groups:
113	
114	- **scipy.stats.spearmanr default: two-tailed** (asymptotic approximation, unreliable for n=4)
115	- **Exact permutation (4! = 24 permutations):**
116	  - Two-tailed: p = 2/24 = **0.0833**
117	  - One-tailed (H1: ρ > 0): p = 1/24 = **0.0417**
118	- **Our hypothesis is directional** (D increases with sparsity), so one-tailed p = 0.0417 is appropriate
119	- **Previously reported p = 0.042 is confirmed as the one-tailed exact value**
120	
121	All 4 groups (DS imp, DS rand, QW imp, QW rand) show ρ = 1.0 with one-tailed p = 0.042. Combined with the JT test results (all p < 1e-9), dose-response is firmly established.
122	
123	---
124	
125	## Summary for Reviewer Response
126	
127	1. **CI Shrinkage**: 2.5–5.9× narrower CIs at n=500 vs n=50, directly demonstrating precision gain without post-hoc power analysis.
128	2. **BH-FDR**: Recovers 5 borderline tests (including DS random s025/s030, q < 0.002). Original Bonferroni with 48 tests was overly conservative.
129	3. **JT Test**: All groups p < 1e-9 for monotonic dose-response, resolving the concern that 4-point Spearman (p=0.042) may be insufficient.
130	4. **G Bootstrap CI**: Importance masking shows 22–148× amplification in low vs high phases (all CIs exclude 1). Random masking G 0.5–1.3, statistically significant in 6/8 conditions but practically negligible (< 1.4×).
131	5. **Spearman**: Confirmed one-tailed exact p = 0.042 (= 1/24). Directional hypothesis is appropriate.
132
